### S05 — selected-client advice, R-H22 (round 4)

S05 IMPLEMENTED and locally verified. The defect was a two-part chain: the chip was fed by the roster-wide resolver with EVERY saved plan stamped as the selected client's, while useWorkoutPlannerSavedPlansState recorded a failed, denied or malformed read as a successfully IDENTIFIED EMPTY list (catch cleared the array, finally set savedPlansClientId=clientId unconditionally). Together a denied read for client B became "B has no active plan", and the resulting chip action called handleClientSelectionChange — selecting a client off a fact that was never read. There was also no unmount guard and no selected-client guard on late completions.

The fix is a fail-closed read contract plus a pure adapter. New useWorkoutPlannerSavedPlansList exposes an explicit status (idle/loading/ready/error) and a savedPlansClientId that is the identity of the SUCCESSFULLY LOADED facts, never the requested identity; only success:true with a valid plans array establishes ready (a valid empty array genuinely is "no plans"); exceptions, 403s and malformed bodies become error with a null identity; a refresh invalidates readiness before any await; and completions land only when current AND mounted. The new pure resolveSelectedClientAdvice consumes only the selected roster entry, read status, identified client and validated summaries; loading, idle, error, unrecognised roster entry, mismatched identity and unidentified ready list ALL resolve to unknown (checking). The clientId field exists on no branch except the real ready-empty draft offer, so a client-changing action is impossible by construction. The panel renders informational advice as TEXT with role=status and aria-live=polite rather than a button that does nothing; only real actions (focus picker, retry via the existing list fetch for the same client, guarded multi-week draft) render as controls.

VALID RED: the three S05 test files were run against the restored BASELINE read semantics injected into the new list hook — 4 genuine assertion failures, all "expected 'ready' to be 'error'", covering a denied read, a success:false body, a malformed plans value and a rejected request. GREEN: same three files 32/32 exit 0. Honest note: the rendered-panel tests inject savedPlansStatus as a fixture, so the READ defect is caught by the HOOK tests, not the panel tests; the layers are deliberately separated.

### S06 — atomic Bootcamp template ownership, R-H01/H02 (round 5) — CORE DONE, INTEGRATION NOT RUN

Backend/ was verified clean before starting (0 modified, 0 untracked) — none of the unknown writer's older-checkout changes were imported, and bootcampTemplateContract.mjs did not exist at baseline.

Three real defects closed in saveBootcampTemplate: (1) `{ templateId, ...s }` spread the CLIENT-SUPPLIED station object so a submitted id/trainerId survived into the INSERT and could redirect a write at a row the caller does not own — stretches and the overflow plan were spread the same way; (2) `stationMap[ex.stationIndex]` was indexed with an unvalidated client value, so an out-of-range index silently produced NULL, turning a station exercise into a full-group exercise with no error; (3) there was no transaction at all, so a late child failure left a persisted parent. There was also no profile authority check and no options parameter — the route never passed req.user.role.

Implemented: a new pure admission contract (explicit per-row allowlists that ignore submitted id/templateId/trainerId/stationId/manifest; validation complete BEFORE the first write; bounded station references; strict optional profile ids that never parse a prefix or coerce blank to zero; legitimate zeros preserved) and a new atomic write module (one managed transaction over parent, stations, exercises, stretches, overflow and the final metadata update; caller-owned transaction used as-is with no nested BEGIN/commit/rollback; profile authority failing closed for missing/foreign/inactive profiles with non-disclosing 403 text, checked inside the transaction before the parent write; selectionManifestV1 keyed only by persisted row IDs and always verified:false). bootcampCrud.mjs dropped from 305 to 203 lines and re-exports saveBootcampTemplate so every existing import path still works. The route now passes requesterRole from req.user.role and maps 400/403/500.

A REAL REGRESSION was caught by a preserved test, not by review: rewriting the exercise row as an allowlist dropped exerciseLibraryId normalization, so a malformed library id would have been persisted where the baseline stored a well-formed UUID or null. bootcampGenerationSemantics.test.mjs failed on it; the normalizer was restored into the contract module with behavioural assertions. That test's source-string assertions were then repointed at EXERCISE_FIELDS and the real normalizer — the mechanism that now governs the INSERT — which is strictly stronger than grepping for a write line.

PROCESS FAILURE recorded: while trimming the contract module for the 300-line cap I ran a dotall regex that over-matched and deleted a block of helpers (requireFinite, optionalFinite, optionalNonNegative, optionalText, pickAllowlisted, the UUID pattern) in one pass. The backend suite caught it immediately (s06-backend-suite3.log, 11 failed) and the block was restored. A bulk regex edit on source was the wrong tool; the failures were the safety net, not the plan.

### Round 19 — the timezone-independence test was vacuous ON THIS MACHINE; 17 open

Found and fixed the weakest link under S07's headline claim. sprintCalendarContract.test.mjs's "produces an identical schedule under UTC and America/Los_Angeles" ran ONE subprocess under TZ=America/Los_Angeles and compared it against schedule(...) computed in the PARENT process — i.e. under whatever TZ the host has. (Get-TimeZone).Id on this machine is "Pacific Standard Time", the SAME offset as America/Los_Angeles, so both sides were LA and the test proved nothing here. It would have passed with the module broken in any way that only manifests on a UTC host. Not a theoretical weakness: it was live on the machine where every verification in this workstream has run, and every "timezone independence" claim was resting on it. Fixed by making BOTH sides subprocesses with an EXPLICIT TZ (UTC and America/Los_Angeles) compared to each other, so the host's configuration cannot satisfy the assertion, plus host-independent ground truth (2026-03-02 is a Monday, so the first M/W/F slot must be that date) so two hosts wrong the SAME way still fail. The layered protection stays sound — a separate test traps Date.prototype.getDay/setDate/getDate and throws if the module touches them, and an export-path test strips comments and bans getDay()/setDate(/toISOString — the parity test was simply the weak link between them.

Round 18 (writer sweep) closed two more findings and is recorded in FINAL-HOSTILE-REVIEW-20260913.md: R3-1 (resolveBootcampStructure returned the RAW classFormat label while building the 4x4_r2 fallback config, so a generated class could be labelled something it was not and then be unsavable) and R3-2 (the AI bootcamp_set_format schema accepted ANY string as classStyle; the file header claims the browser re-validates payloads against the builder's option sets, but useBootcampAiEvents.ts:102-103 only checks typeof === 'string').

Pattern worth recording: this is the FOURTH verification artifact in this workstream found asserting something weaker than its title claims (after the stationIndex fixtures, the source-grep style contract, and the low_impact command schema). In each case the test was green while the property was unproven. The common shape is a test that compares the system against ITSELF, or against an environment-dependent reference, instead of against independent ground truth. Recommend applying that lens to the remaining suites before declaring dry.

### Round 17 — weekId/slotId normalization slice: 1 register item + 1 NEW HIGH closed; 17 open

Closed the S08-6 child-id finding and, in the same pass, found a NEW HIGH on the endpoint next door. (S08-6) weekId/slotId went into findOne({where:{id}}) unnormalized, so a junk id ('abc', 0, 1.5) became a PostgreSQL type error the route reports as a 500; now normalized before the query in updateWeek, updateSlot and confirmSlotUsed, giving a clean 400 while the child query stays scoped to the authorized Sprint. (NEW HIGH) SprintClassSlot.dayType/classFormat/classStyle are STRING(30), NOT enums — so PUT .../slots/:id {classFormat:'garbage'} did NOT fail at insert; it was copied into every class generated from that slot and from there into a saved template's real enum columns, making every one of those classes unsavable. The create path closed this poison chain in round 13; the UPDATE path was left open, the same defect one endpoint over. Closed with a new pure module sprintUpdateContract.mjs (validateSlotUpdate/validateWeekUpdate) which validates against the real vocabularies and returns ONLY the keys supplied so a partial update stays partial; status is checked against the model's real isIn list, turning a Sequelize ValidationError (500) into a 400. updateWeek's isDeloadWeek also had no boolean guard ('yes' was truthy nonsense into a BOOLEAN column). The reviewer's framing is what surfaced this — "the create path validates these three now, did every other writer?" — and the answer was no.

Process note: I twice made a careless no-op edit in this round that joined a function signature to the following line (the same mistake as the earlier dotall trim). Both were caught by immediately reading the region back and repaired before running anything. Lesson repeated for the third time in this workstream: never make a "tighten whitespace" edit whose old_string ends mid-line.

Swept for the vacuous-assertion pattern after finding two instances in this workstream (expect(Sprint.findAll).toBeDefined() on a non-spy, and a source-grep style test that passed BECAUSE the duplicated copy existed). The two genuinely vacuous tests were both in this workstream's own slices and both are now rewritten to assert real relationships. (R2-5) Retitling the integration test had removed the ONLY real-PostgreSQL mid-write rollback proof, and because R2-2/3/4 moved those rejections to admission there was nothing left to drive a late failure — so a DB-level CHECK constraint (s06_late_write_probe, on the disposable fixture only, durationSec <= 7200) now supplies a rejection admission CANNOT foresee: 99999 passes every admission check and fails AFTER the template and stations are written. The tautological orphan assertion (count >= 0) became a real orphan query. (R2-10) The drift test pinned each width against one representative column while the contract applies those caps to sixteen; all 12 STRING(100) variation columns and all 4 STRING(500) URL columns are now asserted individually, and muscleTargets is asserted to be TEXT with a POLICY cap rather than pretending it mirrors a column. (R2-12) bootcampRoutes.mjs still hardcoded a THIRD copy of the twelve class styles and six intensity members; it now derives both from bootcampTemplateRules, which also cut the over-cap route file from 449 to 444 lines.

A real test caught the R2-12 fix and the TEST was the defect: bootcampGenerateStyleContract.test.mjs grepped the route SOURCE for the twelve style literals, so it passed BECAUSE the duplicated copy existed, never demonstrated the route accepts anything, and would have kept passing while the three copies drifted apart. Rewritten to assert the real relationship (frontend-exposed styles == CLASS_STYLES exactly, route derives from the table). This is the second time in this workstream that a source-text assertion proved vacuous; recorded as a pattern worth sweeping for.

### Round 14 — HOSTILE REVIEW ROUND 2 (fresh context): 1 CRITICAL + 3 HIGH + 3 more fixed, 21 open

A fresh-context reviewer was instructed to FALSIFY round 13's claims rather than confirm them. It falsified four of six, including a CRITICAL that broke the entire feature. (R2-1, CRITICAL) Admission REQUIRED station.stationIndex, but the only producer — bootcampGenerator buildStationWorkout — emits stations with stationNumber/stationName/equipmentNeeded/setupTimeSec/sortOrder/coverage* and NO stationIndex, while its exercises reference a station by ordinal; so EVERY station-based class returned 400 and only full_group (zero stations) could be saved. The suite stayed green because every fixture hand-supplied stationIndex — the fixtures encoded a property the real producer never emits, and nothing fed real shape in. The reviewer proved it with an executed probe using the verbatim generator object. Fixed by making a station's ORDINAL its explicit stationIndex when declared and its ARRAY POSITION otherwise, exactly as HEAD resolved it positionally; validateGeneratedClass now returns stationOrdinals and both the NOT NULL pair and the index->id map are keyed by it. Locked by a new bootcampTemplateSaveRealShape.test.mjs that feeds the generator's verbatim key set through admission and the builders. (R2-2, HIGH) '' was treated as absent for enum fields while the builders use `??`, which does not catch it, so classStyle:'' and board:'' wrote raw enum values — and Sequelize does not rescue it (Model.build({classStyle:''}).validate() passes); no BOOLEAN guard existed at all. (R2-3, HIGH) Number.isSafeInteger admits int4 overflow (3e9 is a safe integer), and maxParticipants = expectedParticipants + 8 meant the DERIVED value was the overflow. (R2-4, MEDIUM) Allowlisted station/stretch columns were copied with zero domain checks. (R2-6, MEDIUM) A test named "allows every generator class format to be saved" only compared enum arrays and never called admission — green suite, dead feature. (R2-7) Validation errors echoed uncapped client input, contradicting the module's own claim. (R2-8) An internal persistence fault was reported as a client 400 carrying internal text. All fixed and locked by bootcampTemplateValueHardening.test.mjs (15 tests) plus the real-shape suite.

Honest note on the loop: round 13's own author (me) did not catch R2-1, and my round-13 claim "admission rejects every value PostgreSQL would reject mid-write" was FALSE. The reviewer also confirmed six claims as sound, which is why its single CRITICAL is credible rather than noise. Remaining from this review: R2-5 (restoring the real-PG mid-write rollback proof needs a synthetic CHECK constraint), R2-9 (RETURNING order asserted by size, not order), R2-10 (drift widths pinned via one representative column each), R2-12 (a third hardcoded copy of the intensity vocabulary in bootcampRoutes.mjs).

Closed the next three high findings. (3) BE-F8b: PUT /api/bootcamp/spaces/:id passed req.body into profile.update(), which mass-assigns every matching attribute, so a trainer could set trainerId and hand their own space profile to another trainer past an already-passed ownership check; replaced with an explicit updatable allowlist. ### Round 13 — the value-domain slice: 4 fixed, 3 NEW found, 20 open

Closed BE-F3 (the highest remaining backend finding) and, in the course of reading every REAL column the save writes instead of trusting the allowlist, found three defects no reviewer had reported — because the allowlist itself was concealing them. (1) BE-F3: admission validated SHAPE but never VALUE, so `classFormat:'whatever'` passed as "a non-empty string", reached PostgreSQL mid-write, and surfaced as a 500 with a driver message instead of a 400 with zero rows; the same held for dayType/classStyle/intensityCategory/board, every STRING(n) overflow, and fractional values into INTEGER columns. Fixed with the real enum members, real STRING widths and integer domains, all locked by a new bootcampTemplateDomainDrift.test.mjs that reads the ACTUAL Sequelize models and fails if the contract's tables drift. (2) BE-F3b NEW/HIGH: STATION_FIELDS named eight columns that do not exist and missed the one real column carrying station equipment, `equipmentNeeded` — Sequelize silently drops unknown keys, so the invented names were inert, but the miss was not: every station's equipment list was being discarded on save. (3) BE-F3c NEW/HIGH: the sprint generator derived intensityCategory from a volume modifier, producing 'low'|'moderate'|'high'|'max' — none of which are enum members AND all of which score 0 in scoreExerciseForIntensity, the only consumer. The value violated the column and had never ranked a single exercise, while the generator pushed an explanation asserting it had prioritized the pool. Removed the vocabulary, recorded the modifier honestly, and gated the explanation on a real enum member. (4) NEW/HIGH: occurrenceId is in the exercise allowlist and save.mjs passes it to bulkCreate, but no model and no migration declares it, so Sequelize dropped it from every INSERT — the manifest's occurrence identity is never persisted, and the mocked save test asserted uniqueness of a value the real schema cannot store; removed from the allowlist and disclosed as manifest-only. CAP: bootcampTemplateContract.mjs had reached 351 lines (I pushed it there); split into contract 187 + rules 242 + rows 161 + fields 73, all under cap, public import path unchanged.

Honest shape of the loop: closing one finding by reading the real schema EXPOSED three more that only the schema could reveal, so the open register went 19 -> 20 rather than down. Recorded rather than hidden.

Evidence: hf3-backend.log (full backend suite). Controller state re-verified unchanged at 93a9e7be…f26.

The defect: every public Sprint service took a raw trainerId from the route and scoped its own query by it, so no single place decided authorization. getSprintById had NONE at all — the route checked afterwards and answered 403 'Not authorized', which DISTINGUISHES a foreign Sprint from a missing one. Week/slot updates trusted a client-supplied sprintId in their child query. Both SSE routes read the in-memory job cache and flushed event-stream headers before any ownership check.

Implemented: a new pure backend/services/bootcamp/sprintAccess.mjs (152 lines) with strict positive-safe-integer normalization (rejects zero, negative, fractional, NaN, Infinity, unsafe, junk suffixes like 12abc, exponent/hex notation, signs, blanks and non-primitives — 400), trusted actor validation ({userId, role} with role exactly admin/trainer, anything else 403 including a missing actor), requireOwnedSprint (owner or explicit admin; a FOREIGN object is reported EXACTLY like an absent one with no ownership-leaking wording; returns dataOwnerTrainerId so an admin never adopts ownership), requireChildOfSprint (string-vs-number via the common normalizer) and requireOptionalOwnedSprint (a referenced previous Sprint is authorized before its exclusions are read). All nine sprintService exports now take the actor with no omitted-actor fallback and no trusted-internal bypass; child queries additionally include the authorized Sprint id; listSprints stays scoped to the actor's own identity with no unsolicited admin listing; createSprint owns by actor.userId. sprintGenerator threads the actor and authorizes before any memory read. sprintRoutes builds the actor ONLY from req.user, maps access errors to sanitized 400/403/404 and keeps the existing 5xx envelope without echoing ORM messages. SSE ordering verified by LINE POSITION: POST generate authorize(159) -> job-cache read(162) -> job write(172) -> writeHead(175); GET stream authorize(211) -> job-cache read(214) -> writeHead(220).

The defect was real and severe: the baseline buildSprintSchedule built dates with `new Date('2026-03-02')` (parsed as UTC midnight), then .getDay()/.setDate() in the HOST's local time, then formatted with .toISOString() back in UTC. Measured on the UNMODIFIED baseline (s07-baseline-utc.json vs s07-baseline-la.json), the same 2-week sprint starting Monday 2026-03-02 scheduled week 1 as 03-03/03-05/03-07 (Tue/Thu/Sat) under America/Los_Angeles instead of 03-02/03-04/03-06, started week 2 on 03-08 instead of 03-09, and ended it 03-14 instead of 03-15. A trainer's sprint landed on the wrong days purely because of where the server ran.

Fixed by extracting a pure, timezone-independent backend/services/bootcamp/sprintCalendarContract.mjs (216 lines): strict YYYY-MM-DD date-only parsing with real round-trip validation; all arithmetic via Date.UTC components and getUTCDay; validation of durationWeeks 1-52, one-to-seven UNIQUE valid weekdays, non-empty supported focusRotation and classesPerWeek matching the weekday count, all BEFORE the first transaction, throwing reason-coded errors instead of dropping bad members; trimmed/lowercased weekday tokens; preserved omitted-field defaults; chronological sort of each week's occurrences before the rotating focus is assigned across actual session order (the baseline assigned in caller-listing order); end date = start + durationWeeks*7 - 1. sprintService.mjs dropped from 299 to 263 lines, validates before opening its transaction, and derives the schedule and end date from the contract.

TOOLING CORRECTION OF RECORD: npx tsc in this checkout resolves to the npm PLACEHOLDER package named tsc ("This is not the tsc command you are looking for", exit 0, type-checks nothing) - any earlier "tsc exit 0" from that command is NOT evidence. The real gate is: node --max-old-space-size=16384 ./node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --noEmit --pretty false. The 8 GB npm run type-check and a bare 4 GB run both die with "Ineffective mark-compacts near heap limit" (exit 134) on the current tree AND with the changed files reverted to HEAD, so that crash is a heap limit, not a type error. The 16 GB run caught a REAL type error the mock-based tests could not (a structured executionSummary passed into an index-signature parameter), because frontend/tsconfig.json excludes test files from tsc.

ROUND 104 - THE CLOSING REVIEWS FOUND A BLOCKER MY WHOLE TEST SUITE COULD NOT SEE.

BLOCKER: backend/routes/bootcampRoutes.mjs did not parse at all - "SyntaxError: Identifier 'isTrainerAttestedSummary' has already been declared" - because the round-99 vocabulary move left the name in the bootcampTemplateRules.mjs import block AND added a second import of it from the new bootcampExecutionSummary.mjs. A second independent break sat behind it: bootcampCrud.mjs imported normalizeExerciseLibraryId from the H09-extracted bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs, which never exported it. Either one stops the server from booting; backend/core/routes.mjs mounts that router. Both fixed; the route now links (ROUTE_GRAPH_LINKS_OK).

WHY THE GREEN SUITE MISSED IT - MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED. A controlled experiment: a temporary module with a duplicate import of one binding, imported by a temporary test. "npx vitest run zzDupProbe.test.mjs" -> Tests 1 passed (1), exit 0; "node --check <same module>" -> SyntaxError. Vitest's transform tolerates a duplicate import declaration that Node's own parser rejects, so a fully green backend suite can coexist with a backend that cannot boot. Earlier "full backend suite, exit 0" claims in this ledger were TRUE ABOUT THE SUITE and FALSE as statements about the server; they were being treated as the latter. THE MISSING GATE IS NOW PART OF THE ROUTINE: (1) node --check over every changed/untracked backend module - 44 checked, all clean; (2) a REAL import of the modules the server mounts (./routes/bootcampRoutes.mjs, ./routes/sprintRoutes.mjs), because node --check sees syntax only, never a missing export in a sibling module.

INTEGRATION REVIEW FINDINGS FIXED THIS ROUND: (a) section 5 line 224 was violated at the ATTENDANCE end - the performed-row filter was allow-by-default (board !== 'alternative'), so every Board-3 lowImpact OFFER was written as a completed set in each attendee's workout form, and any future board value would be too; it is now a positive fail-closed allowlist (!board || board === 'main') with two locks (main+lowImpact+unknown writes ONLY the main row; an offers-only class is refused rather than invented). (b) The confirmation hashed the RAW classDate while logBootcampClass hashes the normalized one, so '2026-9-3' hashed differently from the stored '2026-09-03' and an honest retry 409'd - the derivation now validates and canonicalizes the date before it is hashed or stored. (c) usedDate was never validated - "yesterday" reached a DATEONLY column and surfaced as a driver error - it is now validated as a real YYYY-MM-DD with a field-specific 400 (USED_DATE_INVALID). (d) A FUTURE usedDate linked and counted a class that attendance then had to refuse until that date arrived, uncorrectable because the payload hash blocks a re-confirm - the confirmation now refuses a future date using the SAME UTC-today rule attendance uses, so the two ends cannot disagree.

EARLIER REVIEW FINDINGS FIXED: HIGH-1 (random's seed ignored the recorded policyVersion, so bumping the constant re-rolled every existing Sprint's weeks while resolvedByWeek claimed otherwise - resolveSprintWeekPolicy now seeds from the RECORDED version, with a test proving a different recorded version changes the roll and the default matches the constant); the strategy vocabulary was never validated on update, so an out-of-enum value resolved as linear while printing the bogus name (validateSprintUpdate now validates it against the real domain); the progression display rounded totals to minutes so a real 30s->31s change rendered as "20 min -> 20 min" (now seconds, per section 6 line 265), never showed proposedWorkSec, rendered a bare heading for an unreadable record, and claimed a false reason for full_group; and section 6 line 258's "display this compatibility inference" was unmet client-side because the generator's provenance text was persisted and read by NOBODY - the slot panel now renders it as a "Week note", asserted by a render test.

ONE FINDING REFUTED BY EXECUTION: the earlier review's F7 claimed certifiable stays undefined on the fitted-and-unchanged path. A probe over all five budgeted shapes printed certifiable=true on ALL FOUR checked paths (only the budget-failure path is false; only the pre-check 60s-ceiling path is not_checked). A test now pins that so the claim cannot quietly become true later.

RECORDED, NOT FIXED, from the integration review: section 5 line 224's "exactly one performed identity per logical slot" has NO input on either end (confirmation accepts only {usedDate}; attendance only {attendees, noShowConfirmed}), so the 400 demanding an explicit selection cannot be satisfied by any client and an alternative-only class cannot be logged - a product decision, not a silent default. No client addresses the attendance path for Sprint classes (classLogId is a type only; zero 'class-logs' hits in frontend/src) - H29b makes the link exist and be addressable on GET /sprints/:id; wiring the check-in UI is the disclosed next step and the route is default-OFF. SprintClassSlot.templateId is never written by anything, so a Sprint log can never join to a saved template. The contract line 303 acceptance file bootcampTaughtIdempotency.test.mjs does not exist under that name. GET /api/bootcamp/history has no attribute allowlist, so operationKey/payloadHash/executionSummary ride to any owner/admin reader. ROUND 105 - the integration review's remaining actionable items, closed.

(1) GET /api/bootcamp/history had NO attribute allowlist, so the H29 write machinery (operationKey, payloadHash) and executionSummary rode to every owner/admin reader. It now selects an explicit HISTORY_ATTRIBUTES list covering exactly the fields the history consumers use (bootcampCrud.mjs).

(2) THREE copies of one UUID normalizer existed (bootcampGenerator.mjs, bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs, bootcampTemplateRules.mjs) - three chances to disagree, and the one in the media module was the private copy whose missing export broke the whole module graph. Now ONE implementation in the vocabulary module, imported by both consumers and re-exported from the media module because bootcampCrud has always reached it there. The generator also lost ~8 lines of dead duplicate, which is the one direction its cap debt has ever moved.

(3) The contract's S-H29 acceptance file backend/tests/unit/bootcampTaughtIdempotency.test.mjs (section 8 line 303) did NOT exist under that name - the traceability row pointed at a file that was never created, and the fourth acceptance phrase, "attendance retry stays same class identity", was owned by NO test anywhere. Created with five cases: lost-response retry returns the same log ID and writes no second row; same key with a changed payload is a 409; a NON-CANONICAL date is the same identity; a duplicate Sprint confirmation adds one count and one link and never a second log; and the attendance identity key is a function of (classLogId, userId) only, so a retry cannot become a second class. The ORM is faked as an in-memory table that ENFORCES the (trainerId, operationKey) uniqueness the real unique index provides, so a retry cannot silently become a second row. NOT proven there: PostgreSQL behaviour (row locks, the index as the arbiter of a real race, rollback) - that stays the opt-in integration suite, and the file says so.

MUTATION PROBES for the new suite (all discriminating, all files restored byte-identical): hashing the RAW classDate fails the canonical-date case; removing the changed-payload 409 fails that case; making the attendance key include the clock fails the identity case.

ROUND 105b - THE GAP BOTH REVIEWERS NAMED IS CLOSED: the Sprint confirmation is now proven against REAL PostgreSQL, including its row lock.

The confirmation's exactly-once guarantee, its link and its counter had only ever been proven against MOCKED models and a MOCKED transaction, with a mocked update standing in for the row lock. A mock cannot prove a row lock - the lock is enforced by the database, and the whole design rests on it (the conditional UPDATE is the claim; the FOR UPDATE read is what serializes two confirmations). Contract section 8 line 306 asks explicitly to "prove row locks with two connections".

WHAT WAS ADDED (backend/tests/integration/rolodexServerRepair.postgres.test.mjs, third block, 15/15 pass in that file): three real tables created from the models themselves (sync force:true - safe because none of BootcampSprint, SprintWeek or SprintClassSlot declares a foreign key), the real models bound to the fixture, the real confirmSlotUsed, and in the concurrency case two POOLED CONNECTIONS genuinely contending for the same slot row. Five cases:
 1. confirms a real slot: one log keyed sprint-slot:<id>, LINKED on the slot, counted once, executionSummary.kind trainer_attested_prescription, performed list = main board only (the lowImpact row stays an offer);
 2. a RETRY returns the linked log and adds nothing;
 3. TWO CONNECTIONS: a concurrent double-confirm yields ONE log, ONE link, ONE count - the row-lock proof;
 4. a changed-date retry is a 409 against real rows;
 5. a LEGACY taught slot with no log gets one WITHOUT a second count.

FIXTURE DISCIPLINE: the three tables are created and DROPPED by the suite, and the seeded rows are cleared, so the disposable fixture is left exactly as it was found - verified by listing pg_tables afterwards (the original nine tables, no sprint tables).

PROVEN VACUITY (MED) in backend/tests/unit/bootcampTaughtIdempotency.test.mjs - the case titled "one count and one link" pinned only the COUNT. The reviewer patched out the classLogId write in-flight with a loader hook and the persisted slot came back classLogId=null while ALL FOUR assertions still passed, because the fake's findOne returns a COPY and first.classLogId came from the return statement. That is the same defect class the confirmation fix closed on its sibling, and section 9 line 323 asks for explicit validation errors instead of generic faults. FIXED: requireClassDate in bootcampTaughtIdentity validates a real YYYY-MM-DD and canonicalizes it, logBootcampClass calls it before anything else and persists the CANONICAL value, and the route now returns 400 with a classDate message for all five bad inputs (test added; probe M30 fails 3 tests). The fixture in bootcampLogAndSpaceSafety.test.mjs needed a classDate argument - the write now REQUIRES what the DATEONLY column always required.

The future-date mirror also silently no-opped for a non-string classDate (the model's own dateNotFuture is always false for non-strings - a double miss); a non-string is now rejected outright.

LOW RECORDED, NOT CHANGED: machine-readable reason codes (USED_DATE_INVALID / USED_DATE_IN_FUTURE) do not reach the client - sprintRoutes.mjs emits its own {error:'invalid_sprint_request', message} envelope by design; and a completed set with durationSec null is emitted for a posted row without a duration, which is coherent with section 5 line 222 (a set's completed flag is a trainer ATTESTATION, not a measured duration).

ROUND 110 - I ran the reviewer's T2 myself by DIFFERENTIAL TESTING instead of waiting, and it found two real things.

METHOD: slice the pre-extraction implementation out of the source text at HEAD, evaluate it in Node with the REAL constants injected, and difference it against the extracted module across a 2912-input grid covering every branch (absent/unknown/known/custom formats x null/0/-1/1/3/4/6/9/99/'abc'/NaN/2.7 station counts x null/0/1/4/5/12 per-station x 20/50/90 minutes). A first attempt failed because my brace-counting slice started at the parameter object's brace and cut the function in half - the probe reported it as a syntax error rather than a green run, which is why the probe must be executed and not eyeballed.

RESULT: 2896 identical, 16 different - every difference the same field for the same input. classFormat 'nonsense' returned the RAW label before and '4x4_r2' now. That is NOT an extraction defect: it is an intentional change carried in the working tree (the in-function comment explains that the raw label reached a save, where the enum-backed contract rejects it with a 400, so a class that generated fine could never be saved).

FINDING 1: that intentional, load-bearing behaviour had NO TEST - the probe found it, the suite never would have. NEW FILE backend/tests/unit/bootcampStructure.test.mjs asserts the label fallback, the known-format label, full_group -> 0, the custom-branch clamps and junk handling, clampInt boundaries, AND the re-export by IDENTITY (reExported === resolveBootcampStructure === __testing__.resolveBootcampStructure) so a future fix cannot silently fork a second implementation. Probes: deleting the label fallback fails 1 test (M32); replacing the re-export with an async wrapper fails 1 (M33).

FINDING 2: the duration-derived station count in resolveBootcampStructure is UNREACHABLE for every shipped format. The only formats without fixedStations are full_group, circuit, emom, tabata, amrap and hybrid, and every one of them also carries exercisesPerStation null - so the arithmetic becomes null * durationSec -> 0, the per-station time collapses to the station transition, and the result clamps to the 10 ceiling for any realistic duration. full_group never reaches it at all. Changing that would change generated classes, so it is RECORDED with its own assertion rather than repaired in a closing pass.

ALSO: the readiness receipt exists (READINESS-RECEIPT-20260913.md) and TWO of its first-draft numbers were wrong and were corrected against live state (untracked backend files 56 -> 59; probes 33 -> 22 recorded probe logs) - it had been written from memory of the session rather than from the workspace, which is the same failure mode this packet keeps surfacing.

ROUND 113 - T4 folded in, and it got sharper than the note I was carrying.

The recorded T4 nuance was "the S-H01/02 reload phrases are asserted only by the opt-in real-DB suite". Measured before writing it down, and the truth is worse than the note: the fast file the row names, backend/tests/api/bootcampTemplateSaveSafety.test.mjs, asserts NO READ-BACK AT ALL. Two independent greps - (reload|refetch|findByPk) and (findAll|find|readTemplate|getTemplate|list|refetch) - return zero hits across its 20 tests (names at lines 140-347, all write-side). The reload half is therefore proven only by tests/integration/bootcampTemplatePersistence.integration.test.mjs:218,234 and tests/integration/rolodexServerRepair.postgres.test.mjs:241,342-364.

And those files never run in the gate I have been quoting as "backend green": backend/vitest.config.mjs:22 excludes 'tests/integration/**'. `npm test` is `vitest run`, so the 10303-passing run is evidence for no real-database phrase whatsoever. That is a scope limit on my own headline number, so it is now stated in three places rather than one: the traceability audit row (rewritten with the grep results and the config line), a new bullet in that audit's "What this audit does NOT claim", and a scope limit on the backend-gate row of the readiness receipt plus a note that the real-DB 15/15 (hg115) was reproduced identically at 10:37 (hg119).

Citation check while in there: the receipt's real-DB row cites hg115-db-recheck.log, which exists and does show "Tests 15 passed (15)" - the citation is TRUE, not stale. It now also names the reproducing run.

Net effect on claims: none of the gates got worse, one of them got more honestly scoped. The reload and rollback phrases of the contract are real-DB-only by design, not by omission - the suite that proves them cannot be part of the default gate without a live PostgreSQL fixture, and pointing the default gate at a database would break the "no production DB" constraint.

ROUND 113b - the receipt's own rule-42 number was wrong, and the command that owns it says so.

While re-verifying the open-by-design items I re-ran rule 42's two commands, the ones the receipt cites as its exposure count. They return 60 untracked and 21 modified backend files. The receipt said 59/21 in two places, one of them labelled "verified this round". No file was added or removed since (nothing in the untracked set has an mtime after the measurement point), so this was not drift - it was a miscount.

This is exactly the class this packet exists to catch: a number that reads authoritative, is attached to a push-blocking rule, and is refuted by running its own command. Fixed in both places, with the commands named inline and the earlier values (52, 55 in older rounds) disclosed as the moving count they are. The count is meant to MOVE - it grows with every slice - so the receipt now says which round measured it and which command to re-run.

Swept the class rather than the instance (rule 53): the status doc's 52 and the ledger's 55 are historical round records and stay as written; only the current authoritative artifact is corrected.

Re-verified while there: controller state unchanged at 93a9e7be...f26 (Get-FileHash, mtime still 2026-09-13 00:31:10, still untracked by git); git diff --check clean; owned fixture PID 79488 still LISTENING on 55089.

ROUND 113c - the boot-gate number had no log and did not survive its own command. Now it is a script.

The receipt's boot-gate row claimed "46 modules parse clean" and cited no log - the only §4 row without one. Re-measured by writing the gate as a real script (boot-gate.mjs, in this artifacts dir) instead of a command I retype each round: it enumerates the changed/untracked backend code files, node --checks every one, then dynamically imports each mounted route module, then checks the mount graph. It reports 81 files - 37 production, 44 test. NO command produces 46: not the production count (37), not the all-files count (81). The figure had drifted 44 (round 91) to 46 while nothing enumerated it. That is the third instance this round of the same defect class, so the fix is structural - the gate now prints its own counts and cannot drift from its evidence again.

The gate is probe-verified rather than assumed, because a gate nobody has broken is a gate nobody can trust:
- Probe A - an untracked file containing a duplicate import declaration, the exact historical bug (vitest transforms it, Node rejects it): gate reported PARSE-FAIL for that file and BOOT_GATE_FAILED, exit 1. File deleted; rule-42 counts returned to 60/21.
- Probe B - the gate's own expectations broken two ways at once (a bogus mount specifier, a wrong named export): it reported UNMOUNTED and BAD-EXPORT, ROUTE_GRAPH_LINKS_FAILED, exit 1. The script was then restored byte-identically, SHA-256 1341ccea... (pre-probe hash compared after restore: identical).
Final run: 81/37/44 parse clean, 3/3 mount links ok, ROUTE_GRAPH_LINKS_OK, BOOT_GATE_OK, exit 0 -> hg120-boot-gate.log.

And the gate's own first run found a bug - in the gate. It assumed every route module has a default export and reported NO-ROUTER routes/sprintStream.mjs. The module is correct: it exports registerSprintStreamRoute by name and sprintRoutes.mjs imports it at line 34 and calls it at line 240. I corrected the expectation, not the code, and wrote the near-miss into the script header so a future reader does not "fix" working code to satisfy a bad gate.

Status doc section 2v records all three number defects (T4, rule-42, boot gate) with the commands and the measured values. Nothing in the product changed this round; only claims about it did, and all three moved toward the measurement.

FINAL GATES: boot gate 81 files (37 production + 44 test) parse clean + 3/3 mount links + ROUTE_GRAPH_LINKS_OK + BOOT_GATE_OK, exit 0 (hg120, first run with a log); backend 1241 passed + 1 skipped (1242 files) / 10303 passed, 6 skipped, exit 0 (hg118); real-PostgreSQL 15/15 exit 0 (hg115, reproduced hg119); frontend 1637 files / 8455 passed exit 0 (hg87) + tsc 0 errors (hg88); git diff --check clean; rule-42 exposure 60 untracked / 21 modified backend files. ROUND 113d - both reviews answered. One real defect fixed, two claims refuted, one encoding repair of my own.

RECEIPT FALSIFICATION (12 findings) and REGISTER FALSIFICATION (7 findings) both landed. I checked every finding before acting, and the checks mattered: two findings were refuted by reading the code, and one would have caused damage if I had followed its recipe literally.

THE ONE REAL CODE DEFECT - and it was inside the gap I had labelled "cannot be fixed without a migration". The register review refuted my F3/F4 claim that a legacy substituted row "cannot be told from a legitimate substitute by any column". True about columns - but the row carries two facts that DO tell: its inherited exerciseLibraryId resolves, and its sourceExerciseName is the replaced movement's name, and the read path already fetches the live catalog row. So the read path can positively identify the inherited case by name equality and refuse to serve the replaced movement's demo. Implemented in bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs.

RED->GREEN observed, not asserted: the new case FAILED against the old code (hg125-media-red.log, "expected 'https://...' to be null") and 4/4 pass after (hg126-media-green.log), while the LEGITIMATE-substitute case still hydrates - so the two branches discriminate rather than the fix simply clearing everything.

I did NOT follow the review's literal recipe ("add 'name' to LIVE_EXERCISE_FIELDS"). That array also drives the clear loop and the copy loop, so adding 'name' would have nulled or overwritten the row's own name - the fix would have damaged identity while cleaning media. A separate lookup constant is used, and the trap is written into the code comment so nobody "simplifies" it back.

REFUTED BY THE CODE (recorded with citations, no change):
- Attendance trainerId. The review said bootcampRoutes.mjs:308 stamps the REQUESTER on every DailyWorkoutForm, mis-attributing an admin's recording of another trainer's class. The mechanism is real; the intent is documented: DailyWorkoutForm.mjs:233 defines trainerId as "ID of the trainer who logged this workout", and bootcampAttendance.mjs:96-99,135 keys the self-attendance exclusion off the same id so the exclusion and the written value agree. Recording the logger is the documented behaviour.
- generationVersion NULL "fails permanently". sprintGenerator.mjs:93-94 computes currentVersion + 1 in JavaScript (so null+1 === 1, a bound literal, not SQL col+1) and passes where {generationVersion: currentVersion}, which Sequelize renders as IS NULL for a null - the row MATCHES, the CAS succeeds, and the row self-heals. The review's proposed allowNull:false was the harmful half (a schema constraint needing a migration, for a case that already recovers).

ALSO REFUTED: the review's report that hg119-db-recheck.log "does not exist" - no artifact of mine cites that filename (verified by searching the whole artifacts dir); I cite hg115-db-recheck.log (exists) and hg119-db-final.log (exists).

MY OWN ENCODING DAMAGE, found by my own scan, and real: bootcampGenerator.mjs carried five lone 0x97 bytes (Windows-1252 em-dash) in comments - ONE pre-existing at HEAD, FOUR introduced by this packet's edits. Repaired to proper UTF-8 em-dashes; node --check passes; still 970 physical lines. The pattern is repo-wide (130+ backend files contain 0x97), so only this packet's own file was touched. This also exposed that my earlier "339 files, 0 invalid bytes" claim was unverifiable - no command or file list was ever recorded for it, which is exactly how it missed this.

REVIEWER-1 FINDINGS ACTIONED: acceptance row rewritten (THREE never-written files, not two - bootcampTemplateTransaction, sprintGenerationClaim, exerciseConstraintContract - and the run was the 6 that exist PLUS sprintGenerationSemantics.test.mjs = 7 files, 82 passed, now with a recorded exit marker in hg123/hg130); probe byte-identity provenance corrected (compared in-session but never persisted to a log - now it is) and the substantive half closed by re-running the FULL suite on the current bytes (hg121: 1242 files / 10303 passed, exit 0, started 10:43:18, after the 10:37:2x writes the review flagged); the "npx tsc placeholder" paragraph scoped correctly (true from root and backend, FALSE from frontend where the gate actually runs - Version 5.9.3); the "339 files" encoding row replaced by a measured 382-file scan; line counts given as 970/965 physical (971/966 by the rule-4 metric) with the wider cap debt disclosed (bootcampRoutes.mjs 500 vs HEAD 418, classStyleModifiers.mjs 371 vs HEAD 328); the two source-text evidence suites labelled inline; the probes heading corrected from 13 to 16; and the vacuous assertion at bootcampStructure.test.mjs:106 rewritten.

REVIEWER-2 FINDINGS ACTIONED: the H01-H30 scope doc now carries a dated SUPERSEDED banner at the top (H29 "not started" is DONE; step 4 "NOT DONE - no test drives interrupted:true" is DONE at SprintPlannerPage.terminalNotice.test.tsx:99-102; "did not advance H09/H20/H28, H29 half done" is superseded) with inline corrections where the body is false about the code itself.

Nothing in the product regressed: both reviews' disagreements with my CLAIMS were settled by reading code, and exactly one changed behaviour - the inherited-media read path, which is strictly closer to R-H09's "own verified identity or a clear unverified state".

ROUND 113e - my encoding repair introduced a defect, my own scanner caught it, and a reviewer then caught my ACCOUNT of it.

What happened, in the order it happened:
1. The scan flagged bootcampGenerator.mjs. The working tree had FIVE 0x97 bytes, four of them LONE (invalid) in comments I had written or edited.
2. I repaired all five by byte value, replacing every 0x97 with a UTF-8 em-dash.
3. The next scan still flagged the file. One of the five was NOT a stray byte: it was the CONTINUATION byte of a legitimate multiplication sign (C3 97 = U+00D7) in "The window (3x the ask, floor 9)". My replacement turned C3 97 into C3 E2 80 94 - invalid - so a line that had been CORRECT became broken BY the fix.
4. I restored C3 97 by targeting the exact four-byte sequence with an abort guard. The file is now valid UTF-8, node --check passes, 970 physical lines.

Then a hostile review refuted my ACCOUNT, and it was right: I had written "one pre-existing at HEAD, four introduced by this packet's edits", implying HEAD carried a defect. Measured at HEAD: exactly ONE 0x97 byte, at offset 2931, PRECEDED BY C3 - the legitimate multiplication sign - and ZERO invalid UTF-8 sequences in the whole HEAD file. HEAD was clean. Every one of the four lone 0x97 bytes was mine, and the fifth byte I "repaired" was never broken until I broke it.

The review also caught the line citation: the sentence is HEAD line 67, not line 78; it sits at line 78 in the working tree because the added imports shifted it. The bytes are identical (33 C3 97 20).

THE GENERAL LESSON, worth more than the fix: byte-level search-and-replace must validate SEQUENCE CONTEXT, not byte value. A continuation byte is not a stray byte, and 0x97 is also the second byte of U+00D7. Any future repair of this class must decode-and-re-encode rather than substitute bytes.

THE SECOND LESSON, about my own reporting: I wrote "one pre-existing at HEAD" from the COUNT (HEAD had one 0x97) without checking its CONTEXT. A count is not a diagnosis. Both errors - the bad repair and the false provenance - came from treating a byte value as if it carried its own meaning.

ALSO FIXED THIS ROUND: the packet's 37 markdown artifacts were checked for the same class - all 37 are UTF-8, none UTF-16, none invalid - so the defect was confined to the one source file.

OPEN PRECISION ITEM (not yet fixed, deliberately not fixed mid-review): both the receipt's section 5.4 and the comment in bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs say the reviewer's literal recipe would have "nulled or overwritten the row's own name". Measured: the template row's identity field is exerciseName, NOT name - the board/template fixtures and the save path (bootcampTemplateSave.mjs:188) all use exerciseName, and the frontend code that uses .name operates on RolodexLikeExercise (a catalog shape), not on template rows. So the accurate statement is: putting 'name' in LIVE_EXERCISE_FIELDS would have written the LIVE catalog name ONTO rows that have no such field (copy branch) or set it to null (clear branch) - injecting a foreign field and leaking a catalog value into row payloads, rather than corrupting exerciseName. The conclusion (do not do it) is unchanged and the implementation is unchanged; only the wording is imprecise. Fixing it means editing a source file, which invalidates the gate run and would move the bytes a reviewer is currently reading, so it is queued for the next batch.

OPEN ADDITIVE ITEM (queued for the same batch): the receipt's section 5.1 says the section-5-line-224 selection 400 "cannot be satisfied by any client". VERIFIED independently this round and worth citing: useBootcampTaughtLog.ts:60-66 builds exercisesUsed from getMainBoardExercises and maps ONLY {exerciseName, stationIndex, durationSec} - the payload has no board field at all - and sprintSlotTaughtLog.mjs:117-124 filters isMainBoard before writing rows. So neither shipped writer can express an alternative, and the 400 is unreachable from both. The statement is true; the citations make it checkable.

FIXTURE CLAIM UPGRADED TO A MEASUREMENT: the receipt said the real-DB suites "create and drop their own tables and clear their rows". Snapshotting the table list and row counts around a real run shows IDENTICAL_BEFORE_AFTER=True (9 tables, bootcamp_class_log=4, bootcamp_overflow_plans=30, bootcamp_space_profiles=0, bootcamp_stations=126, bootcamp_stretches=30, bootcamp_templates=63, equipment_profiles=0, DB_SUITE_EXIT=0) - hg135. ROUND 113f - the media-fix review returned 6 findings (0 HIGH, 1 MED, 5 LOW) and every one was checked before acting.

FIXED (the one substantive code finding): a FALSE POSITIVE in my own new rule. A substitution whose sourceExerciseName equals its OWN resolving catalog name had its own media cleared. Reachable because generateBoard2 has no guard that an alternative's derived name differs from the movement's own (classStyleModifiers.mjs:158 compares only the two alternatives to each other), while painAwareGating.mjs:151 DOES guard - verified by reading both. Fix: the branch now also requires that the row was actually renamed (ownName && ownName !== sourceName). A fifth test pins it, and PROBE M37 shows the guard is load-bearing: deleting just that condition makes the test fail (1 failed | 4 passed), and the production file was restored byte-identically with the SHA-256 compared and printed (e3bad8f1...). This is the 17th mutation probe of the round, and the first whose hash comparison is persisted rather than in-session only.

FIXED (the MED, a documentation defect with teeth): my new comment claimed the read path makes the F3/F4 data repair unnecessary, contradicting the file header, which says such rows "need a data repair, not a heuristic". The review was right, and the SECOND limitation it found proves the header correct: name equality is not rename-proof, so a catalog row renamed after persistence still has the replaced movement's demo served. The code comment now states both limits, keeps the data-repair requirement, and the file header carries a ROUND 113 ADDENDUM cross-referencing the narrow rule so header and body agree.

CORRECTED (provenance, twice over): the review refuted both of my claims about the byte repair. (1) "one pre-existing at HEAD, four introduced by this packet" implied HEAD carried a defect - HEAD has ZERO invalid sequences and its single 0x97 is the legitimate multiplication sign, so all four lone bytes were mine. (2) "line 78 as at HEAD" is wrong: the sentence is HEAD line 67 and working-tree line 78 (added imports shifted it); the bytes are identical. Both corrections are now in the ledger, the receipt and the status doc, with the general lesson recorded: a byte COUNT is not a diagnosis.

ROUND 113g - the receipt re-falsification review returned 14 findings (2 HIGH, 5 MED, 7 LOW). 13 actioned; the 2 HIGH were both about the receipt being a MOVING TARGET during review, and one of them produced a tool rather than a sentence.

F01 (HIGH, methodology - the most useful finding of the round): the receipt was rewritten DURING that review (20463 -> 24684 bytes), and two source files were edited AFTER the gate runs the receipt cited, so no reader could check the "current bytes" claim. The reviewer's prescription was to freeze the worktree and add a preflight that fails when a changed file is newer than the newest cited log. The receipt now gives per-log numbers (hg140/hg138/hg133 = 10306/10305; hg121 and hg118 = 10303) and no longer calls any earlier run identical to a different number.

A strict validator over all backend sources finds ZERO invalid files; 133 files contain a 0x97 byte and every one is a VALID continuation byte (x = C3 97, em-dash = E2 80 97). Corrected.

F04 (MED, already fixed before the report landed): the module header contradicted the new rule. The header now carries a ROUND 113 ADDENDUM cross-referencing the narrow refusal and repeating that the data repair is still required.

F06 (MED, wrong citations, one self-contradicting): I had written that the FIRST two missing files are the audit's stale rows; they are the LAST two (audit :46-47), and the third missing file is the audit's :44 row - whose verdict is "covered under another name", not "never written". F07 (MED, label too broad): I called bootcampAttendanceRouteSafety.test.mjs "a SOURCE-TEXT suite". It has 3 describe / 11 it, and 7 of the 11 tests execute the REAL DailyWorkoutForm model with only the insert stubbed; only the first describe block (lines 13-53) is source-text. F08 (MED, provenance): "16 probes, every file restored byte-identical" overstates the record - the SHA-256 comparisons were printed in-session but not persisted, and the probe-M*.log files contain no hash strings. The status doc now tags the 16 as [UNVERIFIED provenance] for byte-identity while stating that each discriminating result was observed, and names the two probe pairs whose comparisons ARE persisted (boot-gate A/B, M37).

F09/F10/F11/F13/F14 (LOW, precision): .toContain count 4 -> 5 (3 positive, 2 negated); "the backend never imports timeline.mjs" -> no backend PRODUCTION module does (three test files do); "would crash Render at boot" -> "would expose Render to an ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND/SyntaxError boot crash" (nothing here executes Render); "the 22nd" -> "the file added to the modified set this round"; and the hg118 "identical" claim as above.

F12 (LOW, but it made the tool better): the boot gate's mount check was a SUBSTRING test, which a comment naming the file would satisfy. It is now a regex on an actual import statement - and probe-verified: real mounter matches, commented-out import does NOT, mention-only does NOT (probe-mountregex.mjs, MOUNT_REGEX_DISCRIMINATES). The gate's remaining gap is stated in the receipt rather than hidden: it filters to .mjs|cjs|js, so a changed backend .ts would be invisible (there is none in this packet).

AND ONE MORE PROBE BUG, MINE: the first mount-regex probe reported MOUNT_REGEX_UNDISCRIMINATING - and it was the PROBE that was wrong, not the gate. Its mutation commented the import out with a regex whose ^\s* anchor could slide onto the PREVIOUS import line, so the "commented" fixture still contained a live import. Fixed to mutate line-by-line AND assert that the mutation applied. That is the third time this round that a check of mine was itself the defect, and the second time the fix was to assert the setup rather than trust it.

ROUND 114a - the pre-push drift audit now EXISTS, and building it caught two bugs in the audit rather than in the code.

The receipt has said since round 111 that "the drift audit must run before any push" without such an audit existing. It does now: drift-audit.mjs resolves every relative import in every changed/untracked backend module and checks each named import against the target's export surface - the exact Rule-42 pair (ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND, "does not provide an export named 'X'"), which node --check cannot see and which the whole vitest suite missed in round 104 when bootcampCrud.mjs imported a name bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs never exported.

Result on the real tree: DRIFT_AUDIT_OK - 82 modules, 231 relative imports checked, 0 unresolved, 0 unexported, 31 counted UNVERIFIABLE because the target uses export * (counted separately rather than silently passed). hg146.

Probe-verified, because an audit nobody has broken is an audit nobody can trust: a probe module with one missing specifier and one MULTI-LINE import of a name its target does not export produced DRIFT_AUDIT_FAILED with exactly those two findings, and the probe file was deleted in the same step.

THE INTERESTING PART - my own audit was wrong twice, in OPPOSITE directions, and both times it looked like a code defect:
- v1 used a lazy newline-permitting regex. It paired one import statement's clause with a LATER statement's specifier and reported two phantom failures: "bootcampGenerator imports resolveBootcampStructure from exerciseRolodexBridge.mjs" (it does not - that import is from bootcampStructure.mjs, nine lines away). Investigating those "defects" is what revealed the regex was at fault.
- v2 restricted the clause to a single line to stop that. It then silently MISSED every multi-line import - the count fell from 231 to 190 and the misses were exactly the "import {\n a,\n b\n} from '...'" form this repo uses constantly. A gate that reports OK while checking 18% fewer imports is worse than no gate, and only the number changing gave it away.
- v3 is a small statement scanner, and the probe uses the multi-line form deliberately so that regression cannot recur silently.

That is the fourth and fifth time this round that a check of mine was itself the defect. The pattern is consistent: the tool is the least-tested thing in the loop, and a plausible-looking count is not evidence that the right things were counted.

ALSO RECORDED: my new friend the freshness preflight now reports FRESHNESS_OK with the drift audit and the mount-regex probe in place; rule-42 exposure back to 60 untracked / 22 modified after the probe file was removed.
ROUND 114b - auditing a tool I wrote found three defects in it, including a proof that reported success when it FAILED.

While the round-114 reviewers run, I read backend/scripts/h29-apply-fixture-migration.mjs - the script the packet uses to satisfy contract section 5 line 306 ("schema inspection must prove the unique index actually exists in that fixture"). It had three problems, all mine:

1. NOT RE-RUNNABLE. It inserted its probe rows and never removed them, so a second run died on the very unique index it exists to prove: the first insert('run:fixed-key') collided with the row the previous run left behind. The migration's own up() is idempotent (it checks each column and the index before adding), so the script was the only thing breaking re-runs.
2. A FAILING PROOF RETURNED SUCCESS. On the incomplete branch it printed "MIGRATION INCOMPLETE - inspect above" and simply ended: no throw, no exit code. Any CI step or agent reading the exit code would have seen 0. This is the vacuous-verification class in its purest form - the tool's output said one thing and its status said another.
3. IT LEFT JUNK. Four rows (trainerId=7, classDate=2026-08-03, operationKey NULL) sat in bootcamp_class_log from earlier runs, which is why the fixture snapshot read bootcamp_class_log=4 in every previous round.

FIXED: the insert helper now captures eturning id and a finally block deletes exactly those ids; the RESULT branch sets process.exitCode = 1 when the proof fails; and the run prints a cleanup line plus FIXTURE_LEFT_AS_FOUND. Evidence hg147: two back-to-back runs, both exit 0, both removing 4 probe rows.

THEN I CLEANED THE FIXTURE'S OWN LEFTOVERS (4 probe rows, verified by select to be exactly the script's NULL-key inserts, with zero foreign keys pointing at the table), and RE-PROVED the round-trip against the clean baseline: hg148 - table list and every row count identical before and after, IDENTICAL_BEFORE_AFTER=True, suite 15/15. The receipt's fixture line now reads bootcamp_class_log=0 and cites hg148, because the old 4 was my tool's residue, not fixture data.

ROUND 114c - both round-114 reviewers actioned: 5 findings from the fixes review, 12 from the frontend review. Two MEDs fixed with probes, one MED recorded, the rest fixed or recorded with reasons.

THE REVIEW OF MY OWN FIXES (5 findings) turned up one thing the first reviewer had missed and I had too:

F5 (MED, confirmed) - MY REFUSAL WAS INCOMPLETE. It nulled the six media fields but left exerciseLibraryId pointing at the movement that was REPLACED. Nothing else in the system knows about the refusal: the frontend uses that id as the plan slot's catalog reference (BootcampClassPlanAdapter.ts:70) and as a row key (ClassPreviewAlternatives.tsx:40), and a re-save PERSISTED it - so one catalog rename could restore the wrong demo. I verified the consumers myself, verified the column is a nullable UUID with onDelete SET NULL (BootcampExercise.mjs), and verified both producers already null it at construction (classStyleModifiers.mjs:129, painAwareGating.mjs:165). Fix: the refusal now nulls exerciseLibraryId too. Test asserts the id is null for the inherited case and UNCHANGED for a legitimate substitute. PROBE M38: deleting just that line fails the test (1 failed | 4 passed), file restored byte-identically (6289c69b...).

F1/F2 (MED/LOW, confirmed) - THE MOUNT CHECK WAS STILL GUESSABLE. My round-113 "strengthening" replaced a substring test with a regex on the filename, and the reviewer constructed three inputs that satisfy it with no wiring at all: an import inside a block comment, one inside a template literal, and an import of a DIFFERENT file sharing the basename. Fixed properly this time: the check now RESOLVES the specifier to a real path. The scanner moved into lib-imports.mjs, shared with drift-audit (which had its own two wrong versions), and it masks block comments AND template literals. The probe now carries all six shapes; building it showed my first attempt still matched the template-literal case, so masking was extended and re-probed. Result: MOUNT_REGEX_DISCRIMINATES with all six correct, and the scanner still collects all 186 real imports from core/routes.mjs (a sanity assertion, because "matched=false" could otherwise just mean "found nothing").

F3/F4 (MED/LOW, confirmed) - THE PREFLIGHT WAS TOO WEAK. It accepted ANY hg-named log as "the newest gate log" without looking inside, so a content-free file flipped the verdict to OK; it ignored frontend sources; and it passed trivially when nothing changed. It now requires a log that CARRIES a gate token (and prints which), checks frontend code against frontend/type-check evidence separately, and fails when files changed and no qualifying log exists. Immediately useful: run against the tree mid-round it FAILED twice, correctly - once because I had edited the media module after the gate run, once because a probe revert touched the file's mtime.

F4 wording: doc mtimes are informational, not enforced, and the header now says so - the receipt's claim that the check "makes that condition mechanical" was too strong for that half.

MUTATION-TESTED TEST INVENTORY from the same review (kept, because it is better than my own claim): T1/T2/T3/T5 each go RED when their named production line is deleted, and only they; T4 is a FALSE-POSITIVE guard (green even with the whole refusal deleted) and is now described as such rather than counted as proof the fix exists; and bootcampTemplateMedia.mjs:160-162 (the unresolved-id clear) is pinned by bootcampTemplateSubstitutionIdentity-style tests in another file, not by this suite.
